**Перевод:**

**There are four types of friends: one related by blood, related by marriage, a friend of the family, and who protects one in distress.**

**Комментарий:**

*tad atra bhavatā sva-gṛha-nirviśeṣeṇa sthīyatām tac chrutvā mṛgaḥ sānando bhūtvā kṛta-svecchāhāraḥ pānīyaṁ pītvā jalāsanna-vaṭa-taru-cchāyāyām upaviṣṭaḥ*

**“Therefore, just live here. Regard this place as your own home.” Hearing this, the deer was happy. He ate his fill, drank water, and lay in the shade of a Vata tree near the pond.**

*atha mantharo brūte-sakhe mṛga! kena trāsito 'si? asmin nirjane vane kadācit kiṁ vyādhāḥ sañcaranti? mṛgeṇoktam-asti kaliṅga-viṣaye rukmāṅgado nāma nṛpatiḥ sa ca digvijaya-vyāpāra-krameṇa āgatya candrabhāgā-nadī-tīre samāveśita-kaṭako vartate, prātaś ca tenātrāgatya karpūra-saraḥ samīpe bhavitavyam iti vyādhānāṁ mukhāt kiṁvadantī śrūyate tad atrāpi prātar-avasthānaṁ bhaya-hetukam ity ālocya yathā kāryaṁ tathā ārabhyatām tac chrutvā kūrmaḥ sa-bhayam āha-mitra! jalāśayāntaraṁ gacchāmi kāka-mṛgāv api uktavantau-mitra! evam astu! hiraṇyako vimṛśyābravīt-punar jalāśaye prāpte mantharasya kuśalam sthale gacchato 'sya kā vidhā? Yatah—*

**After this, Manthara asked, “My dear friend, whom do you fear? Do hunters wander in this solitary forest?”**  
**The deer replied, “There is a king named Rukmangada in the land of Kalinga. Presently he is on a campaign to conquer the world and is camping on the bank of the river Candrabhaga. I have heard from hunters that he will reach Karpura lake tomorrow morning; thus to remain here is dangerous. Please consider this and take proper action.” Hearing this, the tortoise spoke fearfully, “Friend, I shall go to another lake.”**  
**Both the crow and the deer said, “Yes, you should do that.”**  
**But Hiranyaka thought for a while and said, “Manthara will be safe once inside the lake, but we must consider what will happen while walking enroute, because...”**

### Text 186

*ambhāṁsi jala-jantūnāṁ durgaṁ durga-nivāsinām \|*  
*sva-bhūmiḥ śvāpadādīnāṁ rājñāṁ sainyaṁ paraṁ balam*

**Перевод:**

**Water gives shelter to the aquatics, and a fort to those within; their own terrain gives strength to ferocious animals, and his army gives strength to a king.**

**Комментарий:**

*Athapy upayas cintyatam. Tatha coktam—*

**Yet we should devise some means. It is said...**

### Text 187

*upāyena hi yac chakyaṁ na tac chakyaṁ parākramaiḥ \|*  
*kākī kanaka-sūtreṇa kṛṣṇa-sarpam aghātayat*

**Перевод:**

**What one can achieve by intelligent strategy cannot be attained by mere prowess; the jackal killed an elephant by leading it through a marsh.**

**Комментарий:**

With intelligence, one can devise strategies to combat staunch enemies by first finding their weaknesses. As strong people are generally proud.

they are vulnerable through their pride. In the following story we shall see how the powerful elephant was defeated by the jackal, who exploited the elephant’s over confidence in its power.

Tad yatha — The story is as follows:

## Story 6

*asti brahmāraṇye karpā? ūratilako nāma hastī tam avalokya sarve śṛgālāś cintayanti sma yady ayaṁ kenāpy upāyena miryate, tadāsmākam etena dehena māsa-catuṣṭayasya svecchā-bhojanaṁ bhavet tatas tan-madhyād ekena vṛddha-śṛgālena pratijñā kṛtā mayā buddhi-prabhāvād asya maraṇaṁ sādhayitavyam anantaraṁ sa vañcakaḥ karpūratilaka-samīpaṁ gatvā sāṣṭāṅga-pātaṁ praṇamyovāca-deva! dṛṣṭi-prasādaṁ kuru hastī brūte-kas tvam? kutaḥ samāyātaḥ? so 'vadat-jambuko 'haṁ sarvair vana-vāsibhiḥ paśubhir militvā bhavat-sakāśaṁ prasthāpitaḥ yad vinā rājñā sthātuṁ na yuktam tad atrāṭavī-rājye'bhiṣektuṁ bhavān sarva-svāmi-guṇopeto nirūpitaḥ  yataḥ—*

**Перевод:**

**There was an elephant named Karpuratilaka in the Brahmaranya forest. Upon seeing him, all the jackals thought, “If somehow this elephant is killed, then we can happily feast on his flesh for four months.” Among them one old jackal vowed, “By my cunning intelligence I shall bring about his death.” Afterwards that cheater approached Karpuratilaka, offered obeisances by falling like a stick on the ground, and said, “O Lord, please glance mercifully upon me.”**  
**The elephant asked, “Who are you and where have you come from?” The jackal replied, “I am a jackal acting as a messenger from all the residents of the forest. Since it is not proper to live without a king, they have decided to establish Your honor, endowed with all royal qualities, as the ruler of this forest, because..**

There is a saying, “Too much devotion is the sign of a thief.” Here the jackal is displaying too much devotion. A discriminating person would be immediately suspicious. The elephant, as we shall see, is unsuspecting. He is the perfect target for the wily jackal.

This story illustrates how the cunning can exploit anyone with personal ambition. The jackal projects a humble demeanor, which is appealing to the proud, then he uses flattery, which is the other weakness of the proud. One who is overly attached to being liked or respected can be controlled by the cunning of persons who know how to exploit this weakness. The balloon of, pride gets inflated by the hot air of flattery, and the hapless victim soon loses sight of the ground. The story of the emperor’s new clothes is a classic example of how flattery manipulated the emperor to believe himself well-dressed, when he was actually naked. When the small boy did not join in the game of flattering the emperor and pointed out he had no clothes, the emperor’s balloon was punctured. The genuinely learned are always suspicious of flatterers; otherwise they end up like the emperor or the elephant Karpuratilaka.

### Text 188

*kulācāra-janācārair atiśuddhaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*dhārmiko nīti-kuśalaḥ sa svāmī yujyate bhuvi*

**Перевод:**

**He who is extremely pure in lineage and conduct, who has great prowess, is religious, and well versed in the rules of polity — only he is qualified to be king on this earth.**

**Комментарий:**

Good conduct, adhering to religious principles, and knowledge of civil order are the most important characteristics of a leader. Electing a good leader is impossible, unless common voters know the true qualifications of leaders. Moreover, it is against the nature of qualified persons to contend for election because a qualified person knows the masses are unqualified to judge a real leader of people. He also considers it humiliating to market himself as a commodity in order to get the majority vote.

Ordinary voters have no idea of what qualifications to look for in a leader, so they elect whom they believe will give them the best facility for sense pleasure. The candidates, therefore, promise such pleasure, but being sense enjoyers themselves, when they get elected into office, they use the opportunity to serve themselves, not to serve the voters. Moreover, it is absurd to equate the vote of a dunce and that of a scholar. Hence democracy has failed to supply leaders with the qualifications given in this verse.

aparaṁ ca — Moreover,

### Text 189

*rājānaṁ prathamaṁ vindet tato bhāryāṁ tato dhanam \|*  
*rājany asati loke'smin kuto bhāryā kuto dhanam*

**Перевод:**

**First one should acquire a king, then a wife, and then wealth. If the king does not impose discipline, then how can one’s wife and wealth be safe?**

**Комментарий:**

Where leadership is weak, thieves are strong. Unqualified leaders exploit their position, neglecting to protect their subjects, who, in turn, suffer all kinds of atrocities. At present, in countries where most leaders are corrupt, it is seen that even their own family members are kidnapped or killed, what to speak of the ordinary citizen. In such an undisciplined environment, who can live peacefully?

Anyac ca — Furthermore,

### Text 190

*parjanya iva bhūtānām ādhāraḥ pṛthivī-patiḥ \|*  
*vikale'pi hi parjanye jīvyate na tu bhūpatau*

**Перевод:**

**The king is like a cloud that supports ail life. It may be possible to survive without the clouds, but not without a king.**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse the king is compared to a cloud that protects the people by supplying water, grains, flora, and fauna. If there is insufficient rainfall for some time, people can still somehow survive. Without a king, however, one’s life is uncertain, as one can be harassed by criminals whose only law is “might is right”. Just as the world cannot exist without God, a kingdom cannot flourish without a king.

Lord Kṛṣṇa counts the king as one of His manifestations, naranam ca naradhipam, among human beings I am the King (Bg. 10. 27). Thus the king is called prthivi-pati, the protector of the earth, and is considered the representative of God. As such, it is his primary duty to look after the people’s welfare.

kiṁ ca — Also,

### Text 191

*niyata-viṣaya-vartī prāyaśo daṇḍa-yogāj*  
*jagati para-vaśe'smin durlabhaḥ sādhu-vṛtteḥ \|*  
*kṛśam api vikalaṁ vā vyādhitaṁ vādhanaṁ vā*  
*patim api kula-nārī daṇḍa-bhītyābhyupaiti*

**Перевод:**

**In this world, which is under higher control, one performs his duty mainly out of fear of punishment. Saintly character is very rare. Even a woman of good lineage serves her weak, physically handicapped, diseased, or poor husband owing to fear of punishment.**

**Комментарий:**

Laws are followed either out of fear of punishment or natural inclination. The masses follow laws out of fear, while only a few rare souls have a disciplined nature. Unless one is situated in the mode of goodness, one does not develop a taste for discipline. Under such circumstances, a ruler must enforce discipline. In the absence of a ruler, or if the ruler is weak, there will be chaos in the society. As it is said in Niti-sastra:

*anayaka vinasyanti nasyanti bahunayakah stri-nayaka vinasyanti nasyanti bala-nayakah*

**A kingdom which has no leader, or has a youth, or a woman as a leader, or has too many leaders, will be destroyed.**

It is impossible to rule without punishment. Lord Kṛṣṇa says (Bg. 10. 38) dando damayatam asmi, “Of those who punish, I am the rod of chastisment.” He also descends to punish the miscreants, vinasaya ca duskrtam. The duskrti only understands the language of the rod. So punishment is required, but must be administered justly. If an offender is not punished sufficiently, or if an innocent man is punished, disorder results in society.

The highest activity for human beings, however, is to act out of love, as promulgated by Lord Caitanya. Enforcement of discipline by coercion is unstable, because as soon as there is opportunity, one will flout the law. If, however, the king has a loving relationship with his subjects, as a father with his children, the citizens will not deviate, even if they are enticed to do so. In a state where love is the only law and guiding principle, people will have the natural tendency to act in favor of the king and avoid anything displeasing to him. Such a state will need no prison houses or police stations.

To establish a reign of love is the mission of Lord Caitanya. This is superior to the much sought after Rama-rajya wherein even Lord Rama could not live happily. He had to abandon His wife in advanced pregnancy after being criticized by His own subjects. Incidents such as this would not occur in Gaura-rajya, for there the devotees love the master more than their very selves.

*tad yathā lagna-velā na calati tathā kṛtvā satvaram āgamyatāṁ devena ity uktvā utthāya calitaḥ tato 'sau rājya-lābhākṛṣṭaḥ karpūratilakaḥ śṛgāla-darśita-vartmanā dhāvan mahā-paṅke nimagnaḥ hastinoktam-sakhe śṛgāla! kim adhunā vidheyam? mahā-paṅke patito 'haṁ mriye parāvṛtya paśya! śṛgālena vihasyoktam-deva! mama pucchāgre hastaṁ dattvā uttiṣṭha yasmāt mad-vidhasya vacasi tvayā viśvāsaḥ kṛtaḥ, tasya phalam etat tad anubhūyatām aśaraṇaṁ duḥkham tathā coktam — ’*

**“Therefore, your Lordship should quickly go there before the auspicious moment passes”.**  
**Then the jackal started walking away. Greedy for sovereignty, Karpuratilaka quickly followed the jackal, who led him into a marsh.**  
**The elephant said, “Oh my jackal friend, what shall I do now? I have plunged into the swamp and am dying. Turn back and have a look.”**  
**The jackal said smiling, “O Lord, please grab my tail with your trunk and I’ll pull you out. This is the result of trusting a person like me. Now you must suffer helplessly. As it is said...”**

### Text 192

*yadāsat-saṅga-rahito bhaviṣyasi bhaviṣyasi \|*  
*yadāsajjana-goṣṭhīṣu patiṣyasi patiṣyasi*

**Перевод:**

**You will prosper when free from bad association, but in bad company, you will perish.**

**Комментарий:**

About bad association Lord Caitanya said (Cc. *Madhya* 22. 88):

*asat-saṅga-tyāga, —ei vaiṣṇava-ācāra*  
*'strī-saṅgī'—eka asādhu, 'kṛṣṇābhakta' āra*

**A Vaisnava should always avoid the association of vile people, of which there are two types: those who are attached to enjoying women, and those who are nondevotees.**

A man is known by the company he keeps. Indeed, his company helps him to progress or become degraded. Lord Kṛṣṇa says that a wise man should give up wicked association and seek saintly company, *tato duḥsaṅgam utsṛjya satsu sajjeta buddhimān (Bhag.* 11. 26. 26). In the absence of saintly association, one inevitably becomes degraded. Saintly association is the only boon in this material world, and one should hanker and strive for this, sacrificing everything else. Srila Rupa Gosvami advises that it is better to be surrounded by fire than to keep bad company.

In this connection, Bhaktivinoda Thakura has advised that even among those appearing to be saintly association, one must beware of the *dharma-dhvajis*, who make a show of spirituality, but their real business is to use spiritual life as a vehicle for persona! ambition. They are agents of Kali, *kali-chela,* and are more dangerous than the outright materialists who make no pretense of their attachment to women and sense gratification.

*tato mahā-paṅke nimagno hastī śṛgālair bhakṣitaḥ ato 'haṁ bravīmi-upāyena hi yac chakyam ity ādi*

**Thus the helpless elephant was devoured by jackals. Therefore, I say, “What one can achieve by intelligent strategy cannot be attained by mere strength (Text 187).”**

*tatas tad-dhita-vacanam avadhīrya mahatā bhayena vimugdha iva mantharass taj-jalāśayam utsṛjya pracalitaḥ te'pi hiraṇyakādayaḥ snehād aniṣṭaṁ śaṅkamānās tam anujagmuḥ tataḥ sthale gacchan kenāpi vyādhena vane paryaṭatā sa mantharaḥ prāptaḥ sa ca taṁ gṛhītvā utthāya dhanuṣi baddhvā dhanyo 'smīty abhidhāya bhramaṇa-kleśāt kṣut-pipāsākulaḥ sva-gṛhābhimukhaṁ prayātaḥ atha te mṛga-vāyasa-mūṣikāḥ paraṁ viṣādam upagatāḥ tam anugacchanti sma tato hiraṇyako vilapati—*

**Not heeding that beneficial advice, Manthara started walking away from the reservoir of water as if bewildered by fear. Hiranyaka and the others followed him out of affection, anticipating that some danger may befall him. As the tortoise was walking, he was spotted by a hunter wandering in the forest, who caught him and tied him to his bow, saying, “Oh, how fortunate am I! ”**  
**Fatigued, thirsty, and hungry, the hunter headed home. The deer, crow, and mouse, followed, feeling bereaved. Then Hiranyaka began to wail...**

### Text 193

*ekasya duḥkhasya na yāvad antaṁ gacchāmy ahaṁ pāram ivārṇavasya \|*  
*tāvad dvitīyaṁ samupasthitaṁ me chidreṣv anarthā bahulī-bhavanti*

**Перевод:**

**Barely has one affliction passed, when, like the waves of an ocean, another descends on me. Detecting weakness, distress comes in swarms.**

**Комментарий:**

We commonly experience that misery strikes in a group. The saying “When it rains, it pours” acknowledges this. A single mistake leads to many problems. Generally one stubs his injured toe. Yet one should not be disturbed, because both happiness and distress come of their own accord, as explained earlier (Text 166).

### Text 194

*svabhāvajaṁ tu yan mitraṁ bhāgyenaivābhijāyate \|*  
*tad-akṛtrima-sauhārdam āpatsv api na muñcati*

**Перевод:**

**Only by great fortune does one get a real friend whose natural affection doesn’t recede even in distress.**

### Text 195

*na mātari na dāreṣu na sodarye na cātmaje \|*  
*viśvāsas tādṛśaḥ puṁsāṁ yādṛṅ mitre svabhāvaje*

**Перевод:**

**That trust which one places in a real friend cannot be placed in a mother, wife, brother, or even one’s own son.**

**Комментарий:**

In this day, where a cheating atmosphere prevails, a real friend is almost nonexistent. People speak of love and trust, but they are not lovable or trustworthy and they don’t love or trust anyone. Because Kali-yuga is the age of hypocrisy, the atmosphere of suspicion prevails. Thus it is hard to understand the thrust of this verse that exalts friendship above mother’s love.

*iti muhuḥ vicintya prāha-aho me durdaivam yataḥ —*

**Thinking like this again and again, he sighed, “Oh what bad luck! ’’ Because...**

### Text 196

*sva-karma-santāna-viceṣṭitāni*  
*kālāntarāvarti-śubhāśubhāni \|*  
*ihaiva dṛṣṭāni mayaiva tāni*  
*janmāntarāṇīva daśāntarāṇi*

**Перевод:**

**Just as the various stages of life, such as boyhood, are relived in the next life, so the results of one’s actions, good and bad, arc experienced in the next life; but by misfortune, I experience them now.**

**Комментарий:**

Generally one reaps the results of his present actions in future lives, but Hiranyaka feels as though he is receiving them in the present life.

*athavā ittham evaitat —*

**Or the rule is like this....**

### Text 197

*kāyaḥ saṁnihitāpāyaḥ sampadaḥ padam āpadām \|*  
*samāgamāḥ sāpagamāḥ sarvam utpādi bhaṅguram*

**Перевод:**

**The body is perishable; wealth is the seat of misery; and union with friends ends in separation. The principle is that whatever is created is ephemeral. (Thus, do not lament).**

**Комментарий:**

*punar vimṛśyāha—*

**After thinking for a while he spoke:**

### Text 198

*śokārāti-bhaya-trāṇaṁ prīti-viśrambha-bhājanam \|*  
*kena ratnam idaṁ sṛṣṭaṁ mitram ity akṣara-dvayam*

**Перевод:**

**Who has created this gem composed of two syllables “*****mitra*****”? It protects one from lamentation, enemies, fear, and is the object of affection and trust.**

**Комментарий:**

*Mitra* means friend. Hiranyaka is extolling the virtue of real friendship. A friend consoles us in times of grief, rallys with us against our enemies, bolsters our courage when we succumb to fear and is the recipient of our veneration, affection, and trust. One who finds a single such friend in the course of a lifetime is fortunate. One who has several such gems is indeed rich. Of course, the real task is not to find such friends but to become such a friend oneself.

*kiṁ ca* — Moreover,

### Text 199

*mitraṁ prīti-rasāyanaṁ nayanayor ānandanaṁ cetasaḥ*  
*pātraṁ yat sukha-duḥkhayoḥ samam idaṁ puṇyātmanā labhyate \|*  
*ye cānye suhṛdaḥ samṛddhi-samaye dravyābhilāṣākulās*  
*te sarvatra milanti tattva-nikaṣa-grāvā tu teṣāṁ vipat*

**Перевод:**

**Only the pious get a friend who is like a tonic of love for the eyes, bliss for the heart, and who shares one’s happiness and distress equally. Those friends who hanker for one’s wealth are easily found. The litmus test for such selfish friends is the time of distress.**

**Комментарий:**

The term “fair-weather friend” has been coined to denote the so-called friend who deserts one in times of distress. Such friends are as commonplace as pebbles on the ground. A genuine friend as described previously is very rarely found.

*iti bahu vilapya hiraṇyakaś citrāṅga-laghupatanakāv āha-yāvad ayaṁ vyādho vanān na niḥsarati, tāvan mantharaṁ mocayituṁ yatnaḥ kriyatām tāv ūcatuḥ-satvaraṁ yathā-kāryam upadiśa hiraṇyako brūte-citrāṅgo jala-samīpaṁ gatvā mṛtam ivātmānaṁ niśceṣṭaṁ darśayatu kākaś ca tasyopari sthitvā cañcvā kim api vilikhatu nūnam anena lubdhakena mṛga-māṁsārthinā tatra kacchapaṁ parityajya sarvaraṁ gantavyam tato 'haṁ mantharasya bandhanaṁ chetsyāmi sannihite lubdhake bhavadbhyāṁ palāyitavyam*

**Lamenting in various ways, Hiranyaka said to Citranga and Laghupatanaka, “While the hunter is still in the forest, we should try to free Manthara.”**  
**They both said, “Please tell us what to do.”**  
**Hiranyaka said, “Citranga, go near the lake and pretend to be dead. Laghupatanaka, alight on Citranga and peck at him with your beak. Desiring meat, the hunter will certainly put down Manthara and approach you. When the hunter approaches, I will free Manthara. Then you get up and flee.**

*tataś citrāṅga-laghupatanakābhyāṁ śīghraṁ gatvā tathānuṣṭhite sati sa vyādhaḥ pariśrāntaḥ pānīyaṁ pītvā taror adhastād upaviṣṭaḥ san tathāvidhaṁ mṛgam apaśyat tataḥ kacchapaṁ jala-samīpe nidhāya kartarikām ādāya prahṛṣṭa-manā mṛgāntikaṁ calitaḥ atrāntare hiraṇyakena āgatya mantharasya bandhanaṁ chinnam chinna-bandhanaḥ kūrmaḥ satvaraṁ jalāśayaṁ praviṣṭaḥ sa ca mṛga āsannaṁ taṁ vyādhaṁ vilokyotthāya drutaṁ palāyitaḥ pratyāvṛttya lubdhako yāvat taru-talam āyāti tāvat kūrmam apaśyann acintayat-ucitam evaitat mamāsamīkṣya-kāriṇaḥ yataḥ”*

**Hearing this, Citranga and Laghupatanaka immediately acted. The hunter drank some water, and sat under a tree to rest. He then noticed the deer. Leaving the tortoise near the water, he joyfully walked towards the deer with a big knife. Meanwhile, Hiranyaka freed Manthara, who immediately entered the pond. As the hunter approached, the deer got up and fled. Returning to the tree, the hunter saw the tortoise was gone and reflected, “This is the result of not thinking before acting, because...”**

### Text 200

*yo dhruvāṇi parityajya adhruvāṇi niṣevate \|*  
*dhruvāṇi tasya naśyanti adhruvaṁ naṣṭam eva hi*

**Перевод:**

**One who gives up something certain for something uncertain, loses both. That which is certain is abandoned, and that which is uncertain is already lost.**

**Комментарий:**

A bird in hand is wTorth two in the bush. Since we have the human form of life, we can use it to attain temporary' or permanent goals. But if we work for temporary goals, rejecting the permanent, both are lost. The intelligent work for the permanent goal, which is love of God. Neglecting this, one wastes his human form of life, and he is bound to lament either in this life or the next. More unfortunate is he who is ignorant of his loss. Those who dedicate themselves to temporary goals are no better than animals.

*tato 'sau sva-karma-vaśān nirāśaḥ kaṭakaṁ praviṣṭaḥ mantharādayaś ca sarve muktāpadaḥ sva-sthānaṁ gatvā yathā-sukham āsthitāḥ*

**Disheartened by his actions, the hunter retired to his hamlet, while Manthara and the others lightheartcdly returned to their place and lived happily.**

*atha rāja-putraiḥ sānandam uktam-sarve śrutavantaḥ sukhino vayam siddhaṁ naḥ samīhitam viṣṇu-śarmovāca-etad bhavatām abhilaṣitam api sampannam aparam apīdam astu — ”*

**Hearing these instructions, the princes spoke in delight, “We have heard this and are very happy. Our desire is fulfilled.”**  
**Visnu Sarma said, “In this way your desire has been accomplished. And let the following also happen.”**

### Text 201

*mitraṁ yāntu ca sajjanā janapadair lakṣmīḥ samālabhyatāṁ*  
*bhūpālāḥ paripālayantu vasudhāṁ śaśvat sva-dharme sthitāḥ*  
*āstāṁ mānasa-tuṣṭaye sukṛtināṁ nītir navoḍheva vaḥ*  
*kalyāṇaṁ kurutāṁ janasya bhagavāṁś candrārdha-cūḍāmaṇiḥ*

**Перевод:**

**May the saintly associate with their friends, may the citizens become prosperous, and may the kings, being ever situated in religious principles, protect the earth. Let knowledge of polity delight the heart of good statesmen, like a newlywed bride. May Lord Siva, wearing the moon as his crest jewel, bring auspiciousness to all.**

**Thus ends the First Book of** ***Hitopadesa*** **entitled** ***Mitralabha.***

**Комментарий:**

The author ends the book as he began, by invoking the blessings of Lord Siva for the welfare of humanity. This last verse compares *niti,* or ethics, to a newlywed bride. Generally a new bride tries to delight her husband in every way. Later on she may display other features — sometimes she speaks sweetly or displays bad temper; sometimes she is miserly, and other times charitable, which is a lot like *niti,* because it also shows different faces in different circumstances. How, then, can it be said that *niti* delights like a newlywed bride? The answer is that knowledge of *niti* is so wonderful in its many features that it delights the hearts of men equal to the delight one gets from a newlywed bride.

*maṅgalaṁ bhagavān viṣṇur*  
*maṅgalaṁ madhusūdanaḥ*  
*maṅgalaṁ hṛṣikeśo ‘yaṁ*  
*maṅgalāyatano hariḥ*  
*oṁ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ śāntiḥ om*

Thus ends the *Hitabodhini* Commentary on *Mitralabha*

Some versions have the following verse instead of the above:

*murkho’pi sobhate tavat sabhayam vastra-vestitah tavat ca sobhate murkho yavat kincin na bhasate*

**In an assembly, a fool in fine clothes shines — as long as he remains silent.**

For a detailed description of this story read The glory of Srimad-Bhagavatam by the author”.
